4. **Colors by need.** The best of variants 1 to 3, with no `source_spread` and no `tapped_share` in the detector. A colors break then shows only in `color_sources`, which it moves by 2.01 standard deviations.
5. **One detector per axis.** Each axis fits its own detector over the features its own break moves, against every real list. The detector flags a deck when one of them flags it, and the flag names the axis.
6. **No unseen share.** The best of variants 1 to 5, with no `unseen_share` in the detector (D-668).

**Two risks come with it.** Gate run 2 flagged a cEDH list with 29 lands under a detector of the precons alone (D-485). The great-over-precon bar reads that risk for variants 2 and 3. **The precon bar has three pairs of room**: run 19 reads Commander at 752 of 788, 0.95 asks for 749, and M-10 failed at 748.

Each fit reports the three numbers of D-648, the precon bar per axis, the great-over-precon bar, and the detector's holdout accuracy. It reads the owner's tiers of the ten disputed decks as information: the tier matches and the mean error in rungs (D-659). It also says whether each variant flags deck 22, the one real defect, and on which feature.

Gate: the three numbers of D-648 beside gate run 19, which reads 16 of 25 graded bad and 9 of 25 judge agreement. **If graded bad falls or the judge agreement rises, and no bar falls, PR-40 builds the best variant.** If no variant meets the gate, a rules detector comes next (D-666). It reads Karsten checks for the lands, the curve, and the colors, beside the fitted synergy check.

**The runs, 2026-09-11.** A throwaway patch adds the flags `moved`, `base`, `colors`, `nounseen`, and `axis` through the variable `M11`, and fifteen combinations cover the six variants. Variants 1, 2, 3, and 5 run as named. Variants 4 and 6 run over each combination they name, so no choice comes before the results. The control reproduces gate run 19 in every number.

**The result: no detector variant meets the gate** (F-115, D-675). `moved` passes every bar and grades 17 decks bad, and every fit with `base` fails the great-over-precon bar. Every disputed deck grades bad in all fifteen fits. The cause is the tier rule. The grade adds the detector probability to the bad rung of every deck, so the flag never decides the tier (F-115).

Four more fits change the tier alone, and every bar holds, because every bar reads the score. `tier` gives an unflagged deck the tier of the ladder, and it grades 14 decks bad. `notier` grades 8 decks bad, and deck 22 rises to baseline. **The owner chose the rules detector first, with no change of the tier** (D-675, M-12). `docs/reference/m11-detector-variants-2026-09-11.md` holds every fit.
> *In plain English:* the part of the grader that looks for broken decks learned the wrong lesson. To it, a deck unlike a tournament deck looks broken. This tested several ways to make it look only at what really breaks a deck. None worked, because a small doubt already pulls a deck to the lowest grade.

**M-12: The rules detector, in two designs (F-106, F-107, F-115 to F-117, D-666, D-675 to D-678).** ✅ measured 2026-09-11, and it cost nothing.
No fitted detector of M-11 meets its gate, so the detector reads rules next (D-666). The checks read Commander alone (D-667). A check says "flagged" or "not flagged", so it never tips an unflagged deck to bad (F-115).

- **Lands.** The deck falls short of Karsten's 99-card need by 8 lands or more, or by 10 or more. The need is 31.42 + 3.13 × the average mana value − 0.28 × the cheap draw or ramp. The cheap count follows Karsten's own text rules (TCGplayer, 2022-07-29).
- **Curve.** The average mana value of the nonland cards sits over 4.2, or over 4.4. No source gives this cut.
- **Colors.** The worst color's sources sit under 0.75 or under 0.8 of their Karsten need (`color_sources`, TCGplayer, 2022-08-02).

The grid fits all eight combinations for each design (D-677).

- **Design A**: the rules set the tier, and the fitted detector keeps the score. A flagged deck grades bad, and every other deck takes the tier of the ladder. No bar can move.
- **Design B**: the rules and the fitted synergy check of the `axis` mode of M-11 replace the detector in the score and the tier. A flagged deck scores under the floor, lower the further past its cut. The dump says both bars likely fail (F-116).

**The dump, 2026-09-11.** Every set of the grid flags decks 19 and 22 alone among the built decks. At a shortfall of 10, the lands check flags 4 percent of the top lists. The best set loses 14 own-copy pairs where the bar allows 3. `docs/reference/m12-rules-diagnostic-2026-09-11.md` holds the dump.

**The fits, 2026-09-11.** Design A passes in all eight combinations, and every bar reads as gate run 19. Every A fit grades 9 built decks bad, 10 in agreement, and 5 owner matches. Design B fails in all eight, at 659 to 662 of 788, because its synergy detector flags the precons (F-117). **The owner chose design A at a shortfall of 10, curve 4.4, and colors 0.75** (D-678). It grades 615 real lists bad against 1,003, and 4,141 broken copies against 5,730. `docs/reference/m12-rules-detector-fits-2026-09-11.md` holds the fits.

Gate: each fit reports the three numbers of D-648 beside gate run 19, the broken copies graded bad (D-674), and every bar. The owner picks no fit that lowers a bar. The owner picks the design and the thresholds on the numbers (D-676, D-677), and PR-40 builds the pick.
> *In plain English:* the grader's check for broken decks learned from examples, and it learned the wrong things. This tests checks built from known deck rules instead, such as how many lands and color sources a deck needs. It tested two ways to use them, and one worked.

**PR-40: The detector fix of M-12 (D-666, D-667, D-675 to D-678).** ✅ merged 2026-09-11 (#144).
Design A of M-12 at a land shortfall of 10, an average mana value over 4.4, and color sources under 0.75, for Commander alone (D-678). The checks set the tier, and the fitted detector keeps the score, so no bar moves. When a check flags a deck, the explain mode and the reasons of the grade name the check. The item changes the detector of the quality model, so its pull request reports the three numbers (guardrail 14) and the broken copies graded bad (D-674).

**Gate run 20, 2026-09-11.** A control fit of the parent `edbe1d3` reproduces gate run 19 in every number. Gate run 20 reads PASS, and every bar reads as the control. The built decks graded bad fall from 16 to 9, and the judge agreement rises from 9 to 10. The broken copies graded bad fall from 5,730 to 4,141 of 5,905, and the real lists graded bad from 1,003 to 615. Decks 3, 4, 11, 13, 15, 20, and 21 rise from bad to baseline. Every number matches the fit of M-12. The gate document now carries the count of D-674, and the explain mode prints the rules read.

Gate: a fit of the pull request and a control fit of its parent read every bar the same. The built decks, the agreement, and the broken copies read as M-12 read them. `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* if the test finds a better way to spot broken decks, this makes it the real one.

**M-13: The date cut of PR-41, in free fits (F-108, F-118, D-668, D-682).** ✅ measured 2026-09-11, gate not met, and it cost nothing.
F-118 refutes the cause of F-108: the top lists play Hobbit cards at the rate of older sets. The owner chose to measure the planned cut before a close of PR-41 (D-682). A card is new when its first paper release falls within the cut of the newest great or good list of the format. The cut removes each new card from `card_rate`, `unseen_share`, and `synergy`, in the fit and in the grade. Every other feature reads every card.

- **control**: no cut. It must reproduce gate run 20.
- **cut30-cmd**: 30 days, in Commander alone. The Hobbit sets fall inside the cut.
- **cut90-cmd**: 90 days, in Commander alone. Marvel Super Heroes also falls inside the cut.
- **cut30-all**: 30 days, in every format, as the text of PR-41 reads.

**The fits, 2026-09-11.** The control reproduces gate run 20 in every number. No cut moves a built deck to another tier, so every fit grades 9 decks bad, 10 in agreement, and 5 owner matches. Every bar still passes, and each cut makes one number a little worse. The cut of 30 days loses one precon pair, and the cut of 90 days grades 7 fewer precons right. **The owner closed PR-41 on this evidence** (D-683). `docs/reference/m13-date-cut-fits-2026-09-11.md` holds the fits.

Gate: the control reproduces gate run 20 in every number. A cut passes when one reader number improves, the other holds or improves, and no bar falls (guardrail 15, D-681). The owner reads the fits, and then PR-41 builds the cut or closes.
> *In plain English:* the plan said the grader punishes cards from a brand-new set. A count shows that the top decks already play those cards. This test measures the planned fix anyway, for free, before any code.

**PR-41: The corpus features read a new set as unknown, and not as weak (F-108, D-668).** ⏸ closed 2026-09-11 on its evidence (D-683). F-118 refutes its premise, and no cut of M-13 moves a reader number.
The top lists hold few cards of a set a few weeks old. `synergy` reads a pair they never held as no lift, and `card_rate` reads a card they never held at the prior. So a deck from a new set reads as weak cards that do not work together (decks 19 to 22). The item reads both features over the cards old enough for the top lists to hold, and it leaves out the newer cards. How the fit tells a new card from an unplayed one is the first design question, and the item answers it before any code.

Gate: the three numbers of D-648 beside gate run 20, the gate run of PR-40 (guardrail 14). One reader number improves, the other holds or improves, and no bar falls (guardrail 15, D-681).
> *In plain English:* the grader thinks cards from a brand-new set are bad, because no tournament played them yet. This teaches it that new means unknown, and not weak.

**PR-42: The question gate reruns a missed conversation (F-112, D-671).** ✅ merged 2026-09-12 (#148).
A whole run fails on one classifier read in most runs, and the miss lands on whichever latent gap the classifier trips (F-112). The verdict then says nothing about the change under test. PR-42 reruns each missed conversation two more times inside the same run, and the document writes the miss rate beside the miss. A miss still fails the run (D-671). Every miss joins the finding register. Each one closes with a guard and a turn test that replays the classifier read, as F-113 and F-114 did.

**The build, 2026-09-11.** Only an expectation or a must-not-ask miss plays again. A premature build, a dead end, and a lint finding keep their own bars. The reruns move no count: the catalog-only bar, the lint count, and the miss count read the first play. The run file carries `miss_rate` per missed conversation as information, so `eval-check` never flips on it. A failed rerun reads its error as a miss of that play. A test plays a missed conversation through a fake player, with no provider call.

Gate: a missed conversation reruns twice inside the run, and the document and the run file carry its miss rate. The verdict still fails on any miss, and `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* the gate tests the chat with a model that reads the same message a little differently each time. When a test conversation fails, the gate now tries it twice more and says how often it fails.

**PR-43: The engine reads the deck after the mana pass (F-119, D-684).** ✅ merged 2026-09-12 (#149).

The engine, the precon checks, the profile, and the grade then read one list. The pass never reads the findings, so no card list changes. A whole build through a fake model proves it: the pass trades 8 costly spells for Plains, and the curve line reads the finished list.

Gate: the test fails on the old order and passes on the new one. The generate tests pass, and `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* after the builder fixed a deck's lands, the checks still described the deck from before the fix. Now the checks run after the fix, so what the app says matches the deck you get.

A card with more than one face takes its front face (CR 712.8a, 710.2, 715.2, 722.2a). A split card keeps both halves (CR 709.4c), and a modal double-faced card keeps its land face (CR 712.12, D-687). The owner reads guardrail 15 as a rule for tuning items, so the numbers stand as information (D-689). `docs/reference/pr14b-quality-gate-run21.md` holds the run.

Gate: a test of eight real cards fails on the old types and passes on the new ones. The free shortlists and the three numbers of D-648 read beside their controls (guardrail 14), and `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* a card that turns into a land later counted as a land already, so the deck's land count was wrong. Now each card counts as what it is in your hand.

The judge names the bracket of each deck, and it does not read the bracket of the prompt (D-464).

**PR-45: The build reaches the power of its bracket, in both directions (F-110, F-125, D-693, D-695).** ✅ merged as #165 and #169 (D-701). The judge is its target at brackets 3 to 5 alone (D-699).
D-693 makes a bracket a promise. At brackets 4 and 5, nothing pushes power. No band holds a floor for tutors or fast mana, and the shortlist ranks cards by theme fit and EDHREC rank. A band miss and a content violation go to the reader with no repair turn (D-613). A precon upgrade cuts a kept card that the bracket forbids, and that cut does not count against the 85 percent (D-695). When the pool can not reach the power, the summary names the gap and the cards that close it.

Gate: the gates of PR-45a and PR-45b. The judge bar reads brackets 3 to 5 alone, and brackets 1 and 2 hold to the rules checks and the bands (D-699). The owner reads each plan before any code.
> *In plain English:* when you ask for a strong deck, the builder does not try hard enough today. This is the plan to make each deck match the bracket you pick, up or down.

**PR-45a: The build cuts a card its bracket forbids (F-125, D-695, D-702).** ✅ merged as #165. Its gate holds.
The content check of Commander Spellbook runs inside `assemble`, after the validation and the precon share check (`generate.go` about line 512). A two-card combo, mass land denial, or an extra-turn card that the bracket forbids adds a warning. `profileOnly` then sends the deck to the reader with no repair turn. PR-45a adds a cut right after that read, with code and no model call.

- **What the cut removes.** For each forbidden combo, one card of the combo. For a bracket that forbids mass land denial, each such card. For extra turns, each card over the cap and one card of each extra-turn combo. The cut uses the rules of `content` in `profile.go`, so a near two-card combo reads one speed step slower.
- **Which card of a combo.** Never a commander and never a locked card. First the card that sits in the most forbidden combos. Then a card outside the precon, and then the card that the shortlist ranks lowest. The pool keeps that order: the shortlist sorts by score, and buy-list upgrades follow it.
- **After the cut.** Basic lands fill each slot (D-225). The engine and the profile read the deck again, which is one more Spellbook call under the limit of D-459. An info finding names each cut card.
- **A precon upgrade.** A card the cut removes leaves the base of the share rule, so it does not count against the 85 percent (D-695).

**Correction, 2026-09-14: the pool keeps no rank order (D-702).** The build of PR-45a found two facts. `NewPool` sorts the pool names by the alphabet, and the shortlist groups its cards by role. So the pool records the shortlist score of each card now, and the cut removes the card with the lower score. A card from outside the shortlist has no score, such as a commander, a locked card, or a precon card. The cut removes a scored card before a card with no score.

**The build, 2026-09-14.** `Profiler.ReadForbidden` returns the cards that the bracket forbids, by the rules of the content warnings. `cutForbidden` cuts them, and `assemble` runs the checks again after the basic lands fill the slots. A card that a revision keeps also stays, the same as a locked card. The finding `bracket_cut` names each cut card and the content it held. Tests with a fake classifier cover each rule of the pick, and a build test fails when the cut does not run.

**Bracket gate run 3, 2026-09-14.** The run read prompts 1 to 9 on `6d61700` for $0.7355 over 538 seconds. 9 of 9 decks hold no content violation, sit in every band, and pass every block check. The cut removed Polyraptor from deck 4 at bracket 2, and Marauding Raptor stays. It removed Akki Battle Squad from deck 9 at bracket 3, and Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker stays. Commander Spellbook lists both pairs as two-card combos (read 2026-09-14).

The verdict of run 3 reads FAIL on the judge bar alone. The judge agrees on 3 of 9 decks, and on 2 of 3 at bracket 3. The bar still reads every bracket, and D-699 aims it at brackets 3 to 5. The first finding text said that the bracket forbids the card itself. So the finding now names the content that each cut card held.

Gate: free tests with a fake classifier. A deck with a forbidden combo loses one card of it, and never its commander or a locked card. A basic land fills the slot, and the finding names the cut. A precon upgrade keeps its share, and a deck with no forbidden content does not change. Then a paid bracket gate run over prompts 1 to 9, brackets 1 to 3, reads no content violation, on the owner's word.
> *In plain English:* sometimes a low-bracket deck held a combo that its bracket does not allow, and the app only warned. This cuts one card of the combo and puts a basic land in its place.

**PR-45b: The build reaches the power of brackets 4 and 5 (F-110, F-127, F-130 to F-134, D-693, D-699, D-703, D-704, D-709 to D-715).** ✅ merged as #169. Bracket gate run 6 reads FAIL on the judge bar, and its second judge lane reads 6 of 6 (D-714, D-715).
At brackets 4 and 5 the prompt tells the model that tutors and fast mana have no limit. Both bands have a floor of 0 and no ceiling. The shortlist adds the rate of a card in the top lists at a weight of 0.1, the same at every bracket. No note tells the reader when the pool can not reach the power. PR-45b pulls three levers (D-704).

- **Floors in the bands.** Bracket 5 takes the low quarter of the TopDeck top cut of M-16: 4 tutors, 6 fast mana, and 8 Game Changers. Bracket 4 sits between: 2 tutors, 3 fast mana, and 4 Game Changers, one more than bracket 3 allows. Game Changers take a new band row with a floor and no ceiling, because the rules engine reads only their limit. The floors of mana on turn four move to 4.6 and 4.4 (D-703). The prompt then names each floor, and the profile warns on a miss.
- **A shortlist that reads the bracket.** At brackets 4 and 5 the rate of a card in the top lists weighs more. A high-rate card with no theme signal keeps its full score. A free sweep over the dry-run shortlists of the bracket gate sets the weight. Such a card also stays on the shortlist, and the sweep sets the rate threshold too (F-130, D-707).
- **A gap note.** When the pool can not reach a floor, the code writes one sentence into the summary. It names the gap and the highest-rate shortlist cards that close it, beside the grade sentence (`generate.go` about line 582).

The plan kept a power pass after the build, for a gate that still missed with the three levers. D-805 retires it on bracket gate run 9, because no power count causes the miss.

**Correction, 2026-09-14: the gap note also names cards from outside the pool (D-709).** The plan said that the note fires when the pool can not reach a floor, and that it names shortlist cards. The pool comes from the shortlist, so no shortlist card can close that gap. The note now fires on every floor miss. It names the pool cards that the deck lacks, then legal cards outside the pool. With a collection, each card reads owned or to buy.

**Correction, 2026-09-14: the weight alone can not reach the floors (F-131, D-710, D-711).** The free sweep found that the role caps block the power cards that the keep rate holds. With no pin, only weight 1 met every floor, and 138 on-theme cards left the six lists. A pin lets a power card at the keep rate skip the cap of its role. The weight stays at 0.1, so the lever that weighs the rate more goes unused.

**Correction, 2026-09-14: the first pin dropped fixing lands (F-132, D-712).** The first pin took a place under the total of 300, and the trim dropped the lowest-scored cards, which are lands. 52 fixing lands left the six lists, and bracket gate run 4 built Korvold and Najeela decks of basic lands. A pinned card now adds to the total. The first sweep counted on-theme cards alone, and the sweep now counts the lands and the fixing lands.

**The build, 2026-09-14.**

- `bands.json` holds the floors, with a `game_changer` row at brackets 4 and 5. A missed Game Changer floor warns where `brackets.json` sets no limit. `profile.PowerOf` reads the floors that a card counts toward.
- The generate prompt is version 13. The deck shape block names each floor, and a shortlist line marks each card that counts toward one: "tutor", "fast mana", or "Game Changer". A revision reads no deck shape, so its shortlist marks no card.
- At brackets 4 and 5 the shortlist pins a power card whose rate reaches 0.3. The card stays with no theme signal, keeps its full score, skips the cap of its role, and adds to the total. A pinned Game Changer land skips the land cap as well. A list below bracket 4 scores and caps as before, and a test holds the score.
- The list carries a reserve of the 12 highest-rate cards of each floor in the colors and the sets. The theme, the caps, and the collection do not limit it. The pool holds the reserve cards that it lacks, and the model can not name one.
- The gap note goes into the summary after the grade sentence, with one sentence for each missed floor.
- The mana pass step of a cheaper card offers a card of the job it replaces (F-133, D-713). The pass sums the distance over every band, so at brackets 4 and 5 a step that adds a power card lowers its score.

**The sweep and the lane, 2026-09-14.** `go run ./cmd/bracket-gate -sweep` builds the shortlists of prompts 10 to 15 at 55 points, for no cost. At the default, all 18 floors hold, no on-theme card leaves, and 7 fixing lands leave the Najeela list. `docs/reference/pr45b-rate-sweep-2026-09-14.md` holds every point. With the job fix, the free mana pass lane over deck gate run 19 changes deck 3 alone (`docs/reference/pr45b-manapass-run19-2026-09-14.md`).

**PR-46: The builder drops a commander that the model lists in the 99, and the copy finding names its card (F-124, D-697).** ✅ merged as #161. Its gate holds.
`NewPool` holds the commander, so the model can write the commander into the 99, and `Normalize` keeps the entry. The copy check then counts two copies. Its finding names no card, because the commander entry carries no name. The repair turn reads a finding with no card name, and the block stays (F-124). PR-46 drops each list entry that names a commander in `assemble`, before the basic lands fill the list (D-225). `checkCopies` takes the name from the card index when an entry carries none.

**The build, 2026-09-13.** The drop runs in `assemble` after the format test, so the first build and the repair turn read the same list. `heldPrecon` counts the command zone as held, so a precon upgrade never returns the commander to the list. A rules test covers a commander in the list and two identical backgrounds. On the old code both read ": 2 copies, the limit is 1". A whole build through a fake model keeps the commander out of the list, and the old code fails that test too. The rules and generate tests pass.

**Correction, 2026-09-14 (F-128, D-705).** The premise that the model wrote the commander into the 99 has no proof. Deck 13 of run 2 also moved 7 cards in the mana pass, and the pass can add the commander. PR-46 closes the model path alone, and PR-48 closes the pass.

Gate: a fake model lists the commander in the 99, and the built list holds no entry for the commander and no copy finding. The old code fails that test. A copy finding over a commander entry names the card. `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* sometimes the builder put the commander into the deck a second time. The deck then broke the one-copy rule, and the warning did not say which card. This removes the extra copy and names the card in any warning.

**PR-47: The bracket judge reads the Game Changer flag of each card (F-123, D-696).** ✅ merged as #162. Its gate holds.
`DeckText` sends the name and the role of each card, and the judge names Game Changers from memory. At least 12 of the 15 reasons of bracket gate run 2 name a card that the card data does not flag (F-123). PR-47 marks each card that the card data flags as a Game Changer. The judge still does not read the bracket of the prompt, and it reads no Spellbook content line (D-696).

**The build, 2026-09-13.** `JudgeBracket` reads `bracketDeckText`, which marks each flagged card with "Game Changer", the commander included. The instructions tell the judge to count only the marked cards. `DeckText` stays unmarked, so the tier judge and the plan judge read the same input as before. A test reads the judge input through a scripted client, and it fails on the old `judge.go`.

Gate: a test reads the judge input through a scripted client. Each flagged card carries the mark, the commander included, and `DeckText` stays unmarked.
