**M-15: Calibrate the bracket judge on decks of known bracket (F-126, D-698).** ✅ measured 2026-09-13, gate holds. PR-45 uses the judge at brackets 3 to 5 alone (D-699).
The judge of PR-47 agrees on 3 of 15 decks of bracket gate run 2, and 10 of 15 read bracket 3. It also names a combo that Commander Spellbook does not list (F-126). So PR-45 can not take its targets from the judge until a read shows how the judge reads decks of known bracket. M-15 judges the nine precons of the repository, which the judge's bracket 2 text names as near the strength of a preconstructed deck. It also judges about a dozen cEDH lists from the Topdeck data of the meta store, as anchors of bracket 5. The Wizards revision of 2025-10-21 removed the tie between bracket 2 and precons, so the precon anchor is the judge's own text.

`readDecks` of the re-judge mode already parses a deck section with a header and a card list. So a calibration document in that shape can go through `-rejudge`, and the tool change stays small.

**The build and the lane, 2026-09-13.** `make bracket-calibrate` writes the nine precons as bracket 2 and 12 cEDH lists as bracket 5. A cEDH list is a Topdeck.gg list of tier great, with one commander and 99 cards. It placed 4 or better in a field of 32 or more. The tool reads the document back before it writes, and it calls no model. The lane judged the 21 decks for $0.33, with no judge error.

| Anchor | Judged | Agrees | Game Changers |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9 precons, bracket 2 | 2 five times, 3 four times | 5 of 9 | 0 in each |
| 12 cEDH lists, bracket 5 | 5 eleven times, 3 once | 11 of 12 | 1 to 20 |

- **The judge names bracket 5 for real cEDH lists.** Eleven lists hold 4 to 20 Game Changers, and each reads 5. The one miss, Ghave, Guru of Spores, holds one Game Changer, and the judge reads it as a lightly tuned precon. So that list is a weak anchor, and the pick rule let it through.
- **The line between brackets 2 and 3 is soft.** Four precons with no Game Changer read 3, and the reasons name rituals, Skullclamp, and dual lands. The Wizards revision of 2025-10-21 removed the tie between bracket 2 and precons, so a strong precon can be bracket 3.

So the second read of M-14 holds at brackets 4 and 5, where the judge can name the bracket. At brackets 1 and 2 it is weak evidence, because the judge reads 4 of 9 precons one bracket higher. `docs/reference/pr14a-bracket-calibration-decks-2026-09-13.md` holds the decks, and `docs/reference/pr14a-bracket-calibration-judge-2026-09-13.md` holds the lane.

Gate: the lane judges every calibration deck with no judge error. The read gives the share of precons the judge reads as bracket 2 and the share of cEDH lists it reads as bracket 5. The owner reads it, and then PR-45 names its targets.
**The rejudge of 2026-09-21, with the combo list (D-790, D-791).** The judge reads 3 of 9 precons as bracket 2 and 9 of 12 cEDH lists as bracket 5, 12 of 21 in all. Precons 5 and 7 hold a fast two-card combo that the app forbids at bracket 2. So the judge follows the rules of the app there, and the anchor is the weak part (F-162). `docs/reference/pr14a-bracket-calibration-judge-2026-09-21.md` holds the lane.

**The floor of each precon, 2026-09-21b (D-793 to D-795).** The owner dropped the anchor "a precon is bracket 2", because Wizards removed it on 2025-10-21. The lane now scores a precon against the lowest bracket its rules allow. Judge version 3 states the combo rules of `brackets.json`. The rejudge cost $0.3107, and 10 of 12 cEDH lists agreed. The judge named the combo of precons 4, 5, and 7 and read each one as bracket 3, below its floor of 4. So the gate raises the judge to the floor, and the run then reads PASS. `docs/reference/pr14a-bracket-calibration-judge-2026-09-21b.md` holds the lane before the raise.

> *In plain English:* before the builder aims at the judge, this test checks the judge on decks of a known bracket.

**M-16: The power counts of real lists (F-110, F-127, D-703).** ✅ measured 2026-09-13, for no cost.
PR-45b needs floors for tutors, fast mana, and Game Changers at brackets 4 and 5, and no published table states them (D-463). M-16 reads the lists with `Profiler.Measure`, which measures every feature with no content check and no model. It reads the 21 decks of M-15 and the Commander lists of the local meta store from 2026-08 and 2026-09.

| Group | Lists | Tutors | Fast mana | Game Changers | Mana on turn four |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Precons of the repository | 9 | 0 and 0 | 1 and 1 | 0 and 0 | 4.2 and 4.3 |
| EDHREC average decks | 1,040 | 0 and 1 | 1 and 2 | 0 and 1 | 4.3 and 4.6 |
| TopDeck top cut | 1,491 | 4 and 8 | 6 and 8 | 8 and 12 | 4.6 and 5.1 |
| TopDeck good | 4,223 | 4 and 8 | 6 and 8 | 8 and 12 | 4.6 and 5.1 |

Each cell gives the low quarter and the median. A tutor is a card under the Tagger slug `tutor` that is not a land search. Fast mana is a card that is not a land or a creature, costs 1 or less, and makes mana.

- **The builds at brackets 4 and 5 hold far less power.** Five of the six decks of bracket gate run 2 hold 2 or fewer fast mana. Three in four real cEDH lists hold 6 or more.
- **The bracket 5 floor of mana on turn four is too high** (F-127). The median real cEDH list reads 5.1, under the floor of 5.5. The owner moved the floors to 4.6 and 4.4 (D-703).

Gate: the table covers every group with the profile's own features. PR-45b takes its floors from it.
> *In plain English:* this test counted the power cards in real tournament decks and average decks, for free.

**PR-48: The mana pass never adds a commander to the 99 (F-128, D-705).** ✅ merged as #166. Its gate holds.
`manaCandidates` and `cheapestSpell` build the set of held cards from the 99 alone. The pool holds each commander, so a spell step can add the commander, and the copy check then blocks the deck (F-128). PR-48 counts the command zone as held in every step of the pass.

**The build, 2026-09-14.** `heldIDs` builds the set of held cards from the 99 and the command zone, and `manaCandidates` and `cheapestSpell` read it. `cheaperOfRole` and `rampOf` take the set of `manaCandidates`, so every spell step skips the commander. On the old pass a unit test reads three steps that add the commander, and a build test reads "Mana Legend: 2 copies, the limit is 1". The build test also offers one spare spell, so the pass moves a card on the old code and on the new. Both tests pass on the new pass.

Gate: a fake build that offers the commander as its one spare spell holds no second commander and no copy finding. The old code fails that test. `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* the step that tunes the mana of a deck can add your commander a second time, which breaks the one-copy rule. This makes the step skip your commander.

**PR-49: Every tool builds the shortlist of the app (F-129, D-706, D-708).** ✅ merged as #167. Its gate holds.
The app passes the top-list rate of the quality model into the shortlist. It passes the commander signal into the commander pool of a bracket 4 or 5 request. The bracket gate, the deck gate, the revise gate, and the generate probe passed neither (F-129). So every gate run measured a shortlist the app does not build, and the gate of PR-45b can not read its shortlist lever.

- **Parity.** The four tools pass `MetaBoost`, and the commander pool of the deck gate passes `CommanderSignal`. The bracket gate and the deck gate load the scorer in a dry run too.
- **A guard.** `TestEveryToolBuildsWithTheSignalsOfTheApp` parses each tool and the app. It fails on a shortlist call with no rate, or a commander pool call with no signal. It failed on the old tools at five calls. `candidates-review` builds no deck, so the scan skips it.
- **The dry comparison.** The dry run of each gate builds each shortlist again with no rate. It prints the cards the rate brings in, and the power cards of both lists.

**The build, 2026-09-14.** The comparison reads the local model `20260910T012734Z`. The rate brings in 0 to 21 cards of a bracket gate shortlist, and 0 to 45 of a deck gate shortlist. It raises fast mana on 5 bracket gate shortlists and Game Changers on 4. The Urza shortlist at bracket 4 goes from 2 to 5 fast mana and from 1 to 4 Game Changers. `docs/reference/pr49-shortlist-rate-2026-09-14.md` holds every row.

Gate: the parity test fails on the old tools and passes on the new. The dry run of each gate prints the comparison for nothing. `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* the test tools built their card lists a little differently from the app, so they measured decks the app never makes. Now they build the lists the same way.

**PR-50: A card name matches without its accent (F-135, D-716).** ✅ merged as #172, and deployed on 2026-09-14.
The owner typed "Grima" as a commander, and no card matched, because the card is Gríma, Saruman's Footman. Every name match of the app read the case and a curly quote alone (F-135).

- **Two keys.** The new package `cardname` holds them. `Exact` reads the case, the outer spaces, and a curly quote. `Fold` also removes each accent under NFKD. It reads the em dash, the en dash, the modifier colon, the registered sign, and Æ as the keys a keyboard types.
- **The exact key first.** `cards.Index.ByName` reads the exact key, then the folded key. A folded key that two name keys share finds nothing. `Collisions.FoldNames` counts each one, and the load log prints the count.
- **Every match.** The build pool, the revision, the kept-card check, the commander row, and both searches read the folded key. The deck gate report, the revise gate, and the questions eval read it too.

Gate: each new test fails on the old code and passes on the new, 11 tests in 7 packages. The snapshot of 2026-09-04 holds no shared folded key over 39,967 names. Quality gate run 23 reads PASS, and the three numbers match run 22 (guardrail 14). The fold adds 3 Standard lists to the fit, and no bar and no deck grade moves. `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* a reader who types "Grima" now finds "Gríma". The app still tries the exact name first. It uses the plain-letter name only when that name fits one card, so it never swaps one card for another.

**PR-51: The role row waits for the which-card row (F-136, D-717).** ✅ merged as #173, and deployed on 2026-09-14.
A reader wrote "Grima as commander", and two cards carry the name. The which-card row takes the name and waits for the power (D-606, D-630). The classifier also reports a named card, and `applyFacts` sets the named-card fact again after `applyNames` cleared it. So the role row asked whether that card leads the deck or sits in the 99.

- **The fix.** The role row reads `"commander_unresolved": false` in its `when`, as the `commander` row does. It waits while the which-card row holds a name. A pick sets the commander, and the commander closes the role key.
- **The test.** `TestTheRoleRowWaitsForTheWhichCardRow` replays turn 1 with the classifier flag. It fails on the old catalog and passes on the new.

Gate: the test fails on the old catalog and passes on the new, and every test of the package passes. Question gate run 50 reads PASS at 73 of 74 conversations, as run 49 does, for $0.1938 (D-717). The role row still asks in the same four conversations of the gate. `make verify` passes. Deployed session `l9x5bFTcFgpZ4DOcQpxU` asked the power and the colors on turn 1, and no role question.
> *In plain English:* a reader who named a commander with more than one match got an extra question about the role of the card. The app now waits until the reader picks the card, and the pick answers that question too.

**M-17: Finishers, lands, conditions, and the theme miss, in real and built decks (F-138 to F-143, D-719 to D-727).** ✅ measured 2026-09-14, for no cost, and merged as #177.
PR-52 and PR-53 need floors, and no count of finishers or land classes existed for real lists. The review of the Gríma deck also found a theme miss and cards whose conditions the deck does not meet. `docs/reference/m17-finishers-lands-2026-09-14.md` holds every count.

- **The replay.** The theme "opponent milling cards" matched no card, because `themes.json` holds no row for "milling" (F-142). So the owned-only shortlist held staple roles alone, and the shortlist dropped the mill cards, and not the model. No fetch land reached the shortlist, and Sunken Hollow reached it and the model left it out.
- **Finishers.** The tag trees count cards that win no game, such as Ragavan, Nimble Pilferer in 47 percent of top-cut lists. The curated count reads the nine parent slugs, `blood-artist-ability`, and evasive creatures of power 5 or more. Its median reads 3 in the precons and the EDHREC average decks, and 1 in the TopDeck top cut.
- **Lands.** At the median, a TopDeck list of three colors or more plays 0 basic lands, 8 fetch lands, and 10 to 11 untapped duals. The built decks at bracket 5 play 13 to 26 basic lands.
- **Conditions.** Of the 26 built decks, only the Gríma deck holds fewer creature enablers than the low decile of real lists. Of the four cards that the review named, two fail.

Gate: a dated document holds every count, and the owner reads the floors before PR-52 starts. The gate holds. The owner chose PR-54 first (D-723 to D-725), the floors of PR-53 (D-726), and a record of F-140 (D-727).
> *In plain English:* before the app learns to add finishers and better lands, the session counts how many of each real decks play. Then the targets come from real decks and not from a guess.

**PR-54: Theme words that find their row, and a question when no card matches (F-141 to F-145, D-723 to D-725, D-728 to D-732).** ✅ merged as #179. It came before PR-52.
The match of `theme.go` read a row of `themes.json` by the exact word. So "milling" matched no card, and the Gríma shortlist held staple roles alone (F-142). No reader and no model learned of the miss (F-143).

- **Aliases.** A row of `themes.json` names its other words, such as "steal" for theft and "gaining-life" for lifegain. The load refuses an alias that is a row name or that names two rows.
- **The word-form rule.** A word with no row tries its singular and the stem of its "-ing" form, so "milling" finds mill. A singular word finds its plural row, except a type row: "zombie" keeps the generic rule (D-731, F-144). The two read the same signals since PR-58 (D-769). Two neighbor words join when their hyphenated form finds a row.
- **Two new rows.** The superfriends row reads the card type Planeswalker, a new signal kind (D-729). The land-destruction row keeps the words "land destruction" off the lands row.
- **The question.** When no word of the theme matches a card, the theme row asks for the theme again before the build (D-725, D-728). It reads the cards of the format and the colors. A theme that matches closes the row, and a reply that keeps the theme skips it. The card pool rows never share its turn.
- **Stop words.** Format names, jank words, and "stuff" name no theme. So a request such as "the strongest Modern deck" gets no theme question (F-145).

**M-18: The Gríma replay after PR-54 reads the card conditions again (F-140, F-157, D-755 to D-758).** ✅ measured 2026-09-18, for $0.2350, and merged as #189.
D-727 held F-140 as a record until a replay after PR-54. `docs/reference/m18-grima-conditions-2026-09-18.md` holds every count.

- **The cause is gone.** The theme "opponent milling cards" now matches 823 cards, because the word-form rule reads "milling" as the mill row (D-724). M-17 read 0 cards on theme.
- **The shortlist.** The owned-only shortlist holds 25 synergy cards, 10 threats, and 1 wincon. M-17 read 0 of each. The uncapped build reads the same three counts, so no cap hides a role.
- **The conditions.** Three real builds hold 20, 18, and 24 creature enablers against the line of 11. The deck of the review held 8. Every token and artifact condition holds.
- **The limits.** The export is dated 2026-09-02 and holds no Gríma, so the replay is conservative. No number holds the state of each deck after its repair turn.

Gate: a dated document holds the conditions of the replay, and the owner reads them before F-140 closes. The gate holds. F-140 closes as fixed (D-758), and F-157 opens.
> *In plain English:* the owner complained that a deck played cards that need creatures, beside three creatures. The session builds the same request again and counts. The fault was the theme words, and the theme fix cured it.

Gate: the word list test fails on the old match and passes on the new. A replay of the Gríma request puts on-theme mill cards on the shortlist. The dry deck gate builds the same shortlists for the 25 prompts, or the document names each change. A question gate run reads PASS with the new row, and `make verify` passes.

2026-09-14 (built): the replay reads 823 cards on theme against 0, and 14 of the 14 owned mill cards on the shortlist against 1. The dry deck gate gives the same output on `main` and on the branch. Question gate run 51 read PASS at 75 of 75 conversations for $0.1958, and conversation 110 asked the row (D-730). It also asked the row in 6 conversations that named no theme, and the stop words fix that (F-145). Question gate run 52 read PASS for $0.1951, and the row asked in conversations 108 and 110 alone (D-732). `docs/reference/pr54-theme-words-2026-09-14.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* the app did not know that "milling" means mill, so it built a deck with no mill cards. This change teaches it the other forms of each theme word, and it asks you again when it still finds no card.

**PR-52: Better mana at brackets 4 and 5 (F-139, F-146, F-147, D-720, D-733 to D-736).** ✅ merged as #181 on 2026-09-16. It came after #179, which merged PR-54 (D-723).
`capLands` ranked lands by the deck colors in `produced_mana` and then by popularity, so a fetch land ranked under every dual land. No step of the mana pass swapped a basic land for a dual land.

- **The class.** `profile.LandClassOf` reads the mana abilities of a land. An untapped dual makes two or more deck colors with abilities that cost {T}, or {T} and life. It holds no condition and no drawback. A fetch land, a land that enters untapped on a condition, a land with a condition on its mana, and a tapped dual follow. Each other land comes last. Scryfall lists every color for Lotus Vale and Gemstone Mine, and neither is a dual (D-733).
- **The rank.** A Commander land reads its class first, at every bracket (D-734). Inside a class the deck colors it makes lead, and then an owned land. An unowned land that costs more than the whole budget ranks under every class (D-736). A land of another format keeps the order of D-450.
- **The swap.** At brackets 4 and 5 the mana pass trades a basic land for a better land. The better land is an untapped dual, a fetch land, or a land that enters untapped on a condition. The swap keeps the band score, it lets no color fall under its need, and it holds the budget. It keeps a basic land for each land that reads basic lands. It drops the basic land of the color with the most sources to spare.
- **F-146.** The untapped land step read the first 8 untapped lands of the pool by the alphabet. It ranks them now.
- **F-147.** The profile counted a fetch land as a source of every deck color. It counts the colors that the land can find now (D-735).

Gate: a test fails on the old rank for a fetch land and a tapped dual land. A replay of the Gríma request puts a fetch land on the shortlist. Its mana pass swaps a basic land for a better land of the pool. A whole deck gate run reads PASS against run 19, and the quality gate reports the three numbers (guardrail 14). 2026-09-14 correction (M-17): the replay shortlist already holds Sunken Hollow, so the gate reads a fetch land and the swap.

2026-09-15 (built): every new test fails on the old code. The replay of the Gríma request puts Fabled Passage, Evolving Wilds, and Terramorphic Expanse on the shortlist. The swap takes Fabled Passage and Sunken Hollow, and the reader owns both. 9 of the 25 deck gate pools change, and every one is a Commander pool. Quality gate run 24 reads PASS, and it holds the precon bar and the synergy axis of a control fit of `main`. Built decks graded bad read 10 of 25 against 9, and the judge agreement 10 against 9 (D-689). Deck gate run 23 reads PASS at 25 of 25 decks for $2.6509, and `eval-check` moves no gate row against run 19. Bracket gate run 7 reads the judge at 5 of 6, against 4 of 6 on run 6, and the bar is 80 percent. Both bracket runs read FAIL on the band bar, and the same deck sits off band. `docs/reference/pr52-land-rank-2026-09-15.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* the app picks lands by their colors and their popularity, so it misses the lands that strong decks play. This change ranks lands by how good they are, and it puts your own good lands first.

**PR-53: A win-condition target for Commander decks (F-138, D-719, D-726, D-739, D-742 to D-744).** ✅ merged as #182. #181 merged PR-52 before it.
`roles.go` names a wincon from the tag alternate-win-condition alone, and `TargetsFor` holds no wincon target. So the shortlist rarely labels a finisher, and no check reads a win route.

- **Roles.** The curated count of M-17 reads the nine parent tags with no child tag. It adds the child tag `blood-artist-ability` and the evasive creatures of power 5 or more. `profile.FinisherIDs` holds the set, and the profile counts the same cards. The shortlist gives the role wincon to the target count of them, and every other finisher keeps the role it earned (D-741).
- **The target.** Brackets 1 to 4 take a wincon target of 3, and bracket 5 takes 1 (D-726). The prompt names the target, and the finisher places come out of the synergy pieces. The shortlist pins the finishers up to the target, so no role cap drops the win route (D-739). An owned mode pins the owned finishers first, because it drops the unowned cards after that step (D-742).
- **The count.** The stored profile holds a finisher row. Every Commander bracket carries a floor: 2 at brackets 1 to 4, and 1 at bracket 5. A deck under the floor takes an off-band finding.
- **The gap note.** The summary of a deck under the floor names the finishers that close the gap, and it marks each one owned or to buy.
- **The prompt.** The deck shape block names the finisher floor, and each shortlist line marks a finisher. The prompt version rises to 14.
- **The words.** The finisher note and the finisher finding name the deck plan, and not the bracket. A bracket counts Game Changers, mass land denial, extra turns, and combos, and it counts no finisher (F-151, D-743).
- **The repair.** The shortfall carries the code `finisher_short`, so no repair input holds it. A pool with too few finishers can not close the gap, and the model then answers with no deck (F-152). An empty answer never replaces a deck that holds cards.
- **The leader.** Only the commander leads the deck, and the summary rules forbid the words for any other card (F-153, D-744). The prompt version reads 15.

Gate: a test fails on the old roles for a card tagged drain-life. The prompt version rises, and a whole deck gate run reads PASS against run 19. The bracket gate reads the judge at brackets 4 and 5 again.

2026-09-16 (built): every new test fails on the old code. The Gríma replay reads 3 wincon rows of a target of 3, against 2 before D-742. No deck gate prompt holds fewer finishers than `main`, and 11 of the 25 pools hold the same cards. Deck gate run 24 read FAIL and found F-150 to F-152, and run 25 read FAIL and found F-153 (D-743, D-744). Deck gate run 28 reads PASS at 25 of 25 decks, 0 invented names, and 0 false rules, for $2.7527. `eval-check` moves no gate row against run 19. Bracket gate run 8 reads the judge at 5 of 6, 83 percent against a bar of 80, for $0.5318. Every one of its six decks meets its finisher floor. `docs/reference/pr53-wincon-2026-09-16.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* the app built a deck that had no way to win. This change counts the cards that can win a game, asks the builder for enough of them, and names the missing ones.

**PR-55: A typal land signal on the type rows (F-144, F-148, D-737, D-759 to D-761).** 🔧 built 2026-09-19, as #190. It came after #189, which merged M-18.
The type rows of `themes.json` read a subtype, a typal tag, and a few payoff phrases. None of them reads a land. So a typal shortlist lost every land that makes mana for its type, and F-148 recorded it.

- **The block.** A new `typal_land` block of `themes.json` holds the tag `typal-choose` and the needle "shares a creature type with your commander". The tag reads Cavern of Souls, Secluded Courtyard, Unclaimed Territory, and Three Tree City. The needle reads Path of Ancestry, which names no chosen type.
- **The row.** Every row with a subtype carries the block, and each row adds its own subtype word as a land needle (D-759). That reaches Sliver Hive, Haven of the Spirit Dragon, and Restless Ridgeline.
- **The generic rule.** A singular creature type keeps the generic rule and reads no type row (D-731). The question gate records the theme "dinosaur" and not "dinosaurs", so that word carries the signal too (D-761). The trigger is a `typal-<word>` slug that the card database holds, and the snapshot of 2026-09-04 holds 238 of them.
- **The card type.** The signal counts on a land alone (D-760). The tag also holds 88 nonlands, such as Adaptive Automaton and Coat of Arms, and the owner kept PR-55 to the lands of F-148.
- **The weight.** A typal land counts once, at the payoff-text weight of 1.2 against the score cap of 2.5. The tag and the needles state one fact about one card.
- **The word boundary.** A land needle matches on a word boundary, with an optional plural "s". A short creature type sits in a common word: "bat" in "battlefield", "orc" in "sorcery", and "mount" in "mountain". Those three read 218 of the 1,194 Commander-legal lands. The needle "every creature type" keeps the one changeling land, which rewards every typal deck.
- **The land cap.** `capLands` gives half its places to the mana order and half to the theme lands. A typal land reads a theme signal now, so the theme half keeps it.

Gate: a test fails on the old code for each of the five lands of F-148. A land needle reads no land whose text holds the type inside a longer word. A snapshot test builds the dinosaur typal prompt of the deck gate. It reads the plural and the singular, and it finds all five on the shortlist. `make themes-check` runs it. The signal reads no nonland, and a word that names no creature type carries no land signal.

2026-09-19 (built): every new test fails on the old code. The dinosaur prompt of the deck gate holds all five lands of F-148 and Restless Ridgeline. The theme "dinosaur" read 0 of the five before D-761, and it reads all five now. The shortlist still holds 266 cards and 40 lands, and the on-theme count moves from 208 to 215. Seven lands that enter tapped on a condition leave to make the room. Before the change, every type row read an on-theme count of 1 or more in every single color. So the change hides no theme question of D-725. No paid target ran. `docs/reference/pr55-typal-lands-2026-09-19.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* a tribal deck needs the lands that make mana for its tribe. The app dropped every one of them for a plain dual land. This change teaches each tribe row to recognize its own lands.

**PR-56: A finding on the commander buys no repair turn (F-157, D-762, D-763).** 🔧 built 2026-09-19, as #191. It came after #190, which merged PR-55.
Each of the three M-18 builds ran a repair turn, and the reason of each one names a `not_owned` finding. That finding names the commander the reader named, and the collection of the reader holds no copy of it.

- **The cause.** `assemble` takes the commander from the request on every pass. The answer of the model holds a card list, and that list never reaches the command zone. So a finding on a commander survives every repair turn.
- **The gate.** `fixable` drops every finding that names a commander of the deck, before the build counts what a repair turn can fix. A finding on any other card still buys the turn.
- **The deck.** The deck keeps the finding, and the reader reads the block beside the deck (D-226, D-300). The build writes one log line for the findings it drops.
- **The saving.** An owned-only build with an unowned commander spends one model call now, and not two. A repair call takes 60 to 90 seconds (F-77).
- **The measurement.** A free replay of the M-18 request names the card of the finding. A control run with the commander covered reads profile findings alone, and no repair turn.

Gate: two tests fail on the old code. The first reads the finding shape of the M-18 builds, and it asks for no repair turn. The second builds a whole Commander deck with an unowned commander, and it counts one model call.

2026-09-19 (built): both tests fail on the old code and pass on the new code. The whole `generate` package passes. The owned pool of the replay reaches 1 Game Changer and 1 tutor. Bracket 5 wants 8 and 4, so two floors sit outside its reach (D-763). No paid target ran, so no deck of a model measures the change. `docs/reference/f157-owned-commander-2026-09-19.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* the app saw that your collection holds no copy of your own commander. It then paid the model a second time to repair a deck that no answer can repair. This change stops that second call, which costs money and about a minute.

**PR-57: A typal card signal on the type rows (F-144, D-765 to D-768).** 🔧 built 2026-09-20, as #193. It came after #192, which merged the merge trigger of D-764.
PR-55 taught a type row to read a land that makes mana for its type. A card that is no land kept the old gap. Door of Destinies rewards a creature type the player chooses, it names no type, and its type line names no type. So no signal of the row read it.

- **The block.** The `typal_land` block of `themes.json` is the `typal` block now. It holds `land_slugs` and `land_text` for a land. It holds `card_slugs` for a card that is no land. It holds `noncreature_slugs` for a card that is no land and no creature.
- **The tags.** `card_slugs` holds `typal-choose`, which the card index reads on 85 nonlands, 81 of them legal in Commander. `noncreature_slugs` holds `typal-share`, which holds Coat of Arms and Shared Animosity (D-765).
- **The creature rule.** 40 of the 70 Commander-legal nonlands of `typal-share` are creatures of one named type, such as Kilnmouth Dragon. Each one rewards that type alone, so the tag reads no creature and no Kindred card.
- **The weight.** A typal card counts once, at the payoff-text weight of 1.2 against the score cap of 2.5 (D-766). A payoff tag and the subtype together score 2.3, so a real card of the type still outranks a generic payoff.
- **The generic rule.** A singular creature type keeps the generic rule and reads no type row (D-731). The card signals follow the land signals onto that rule (D-761).
- **The other half.** F-144 stays open for a card whose text names the type (D-768). A plain text needle also reads the cards that punish the type, so that half needs its own rule.

Gate: a test fails on the old code for a generic payoff of each tag. A creature of `typal-share` reads no signal of a row it does not name, and a card that holds both tags scores one payoff text. A snapshot test builds the dinosaur typal prompt of the deck gate, in the plural and in the singular. It finds eight generic payoffs on the shortlist, and `make themes-check` runs it. A row with no subtype carries no card signal. The deck gate measures the deck a model writes (D-767).

2026-09-20 (built): every new test fails on the old code. The dinosaur prompt reads 215 on theme before the change, 268 with `typal-choose`, and 289 with both tags. The shortlist moves from 266 cards to 295, and it holds 40 lands before and after.

Before the change, every type row read an on-theme count of 1 or more in every single color. The generic rule reaches 190 more words, and 5 of their 950 color pairs read 0 before the change and after it. So the change hides no theme question of D-725. Deck gate run 29 reads PASS for $2.7384, and its dinosaur deck holds eight generic typal payoffs. The same deck of run 28 held none. `docs/reference/pr57-typal-nonlands-2026-09-20.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* a tribal deck wants the cards that reward any tribe, such as Door of Destinies and Coat of Arms. The app read them as cards of no theme, so they almost never reached your deck. This change teaches each tribe row to recognize them.

**PR-58: A type row reads a card that names its type (F-144, F-158, D-769, D-770).** 🔧 built 2026-09-20, as #194. It came after #193, which merged PR-57.
PR-55 taught a type row to read a land that names its type, and PR-57 taught it to read a generic typal payoff. A card that only names the type kept the last gap. Army of the Damned makes thirteen Zombie tokens, it carries no Zombie type, and it holds no typal tag. So no signal of the zombies row read it.

- **The needle.** The typal block gives each type row and each generic type word one text needle: the type word itself (D-769). It reads a card that names the type in its text.
- **The word boundary.** The needle matches on a word boundary, as the land needle does (D-759). A text needle of `themes.json` reads a substring. The substring "cat" reads 165 Commander-legal cards, and the 55 that name a Cat.
- **The generic rule.** A singular type word takes this needle and drops its substring text needle (D-769). So "zombie" and "zombies" read the same signals now, and D-731 no longer splits them.
- **The land rule.** The needle counts on a card that is no land. A land reads the same word as a land needle, at the typal weight, so the word counts once (D-759).
- **The weight.** The needle names a type and proves no payoff, so it weighs as text, at 0.4 against the score cap of 2.5. A real card of the type outranks a card that only names one.
- **The target.** `make themes-check` ran three snapshot tests and named two more that no pattern matched (F-158). Its pattern reads `ReachATypalShortlist` now, and the target runs five tests.

Gate: a test fails on the old code for a token maker and for a card that turns a permanent into the type. A card that names no type scores 0, and so does a card that holds "cat" inside "indicate" or "automaton". A card that names a Cat reads the needle. Each type row and its singular word read the same needle, and the singular reads no substring needle. A snapshot test builds a zombie Commander shortlist, in the plural and in the singular, and it finds five cards that name the type. `make themes-check` runs it, and no color of any type row drops to 0 on-theme cards (D-725).

2026-09-20 (built): every new test fails on the old code. The needle gives a signal to 786 cards of the snapshot of 2026-09-04 that no signal reached. Of the 786, 550 make a token of the type and 87 turn a permanent into it. The other 149 are noise: 55 hold the word after "non-" alone, 15 match the card's own name, and 79 are the rest.

A replay of five shortlists reads a swap and no growth: 84 cards entered and 84 left. Of the 84, 46 are a creature of the type and 34 make one. No card that punishes the type entered. The plural and the singular now read one shortlist, card for card, for zombies, goblins, spirits, and cats.

Every type row reads 1 or more on-theme cards in every single color. That holds before the change and after it, so the change hides no theme question of D-725. No paid target ran (D-770). `docs/reference/pr58-type-name-2026-09-20.md` holds every count.
> *In plain English:* a zombie deck wants the cards that make zombies. The app read a card that makes thirteen of them as a card of no theme. This change teaches each tribe row to find its own name in the card text. It also stops the word "cat" in a match with the word "indicate".

**PR-59: The prompt reads the commander (F-159, D-771 to D-774).** 🔧 built 2026-09-20, as #195. It came after #194, which merged PR-58.
The review of the Gríma deck of 2026-09-14 asked whether the build reads the payoff shape of the commander. A free read of the code says it does not, and F-159 records the gap. Gríma, Saruman's Footman can not be blocked, and its trigger fires one time for each hit. The prompt stated none of that. It named the card and stopped.

- **The block.** The prompt writes the name, the type line, the mana cost, the power and toughness, and every line of Oracle text of each commander (D-771).
- **The dash.** The line carries no leading dash. A shortlist line starts with one, and the commander is not on the list (D-302).
- **The format rule.** Only a Commander session reads the block, as only a Commander session reads the commander sentence (D-233).
- **No discount.** The build adds no discount for evasion the commander already holds (D-772). Three cards of a 201-card shortlist carry such a grant, and each carries a second mode.
- **No cap.** A cap per effect class waits for the shape work (D-773). The class weights need the shape, and the shortlist reads none of it.
- **The power counts.** A deck shows the floor counts of its bracket to the reader (D-774). That is a reader-facing change, and it takes its own pull request.

Gate: a Commander session reads the type line, the mana cost, the power and toughness, and each Oracle line of its commander. A Modern session and a Standard session read none of it. A pair reads both cards. A commander the card index holds with no Oracle text reads its name alone, and a commander the index does not hold writes no block. No line of the block starts with a dash, so the shortlist test of D-302 still passes.

2026-09-20 (built): the five new tests fail on the old code. `go test ./internal/generate/` passes, and `TestShortlistOmitsTheCommander` holds. The first draft of the block used the shortlist line format, and that test caught it. So the block now reads a different shape than a shortlist line.

Every count of this item is free, and no paid target ran. `docs/reference/grima-payoff-shape-2026-09-20.md` holds the replay of the M-18 shortlist, the classification of its 10 evasion cards, and the effect-class counts. CAUTION: the change is not deterministic. No test can prove that a deck improves, and the next whole deck gate run carries it beside any other change that lands first.
> *In plain English:* the app told the model the name of the commander and nothing else. The model had to remember the card. This commander cannot be blocked, and it rewards each hit one time. The app now sends the card text, so the model can read what the deck must do.

**PR-60: A deck shows its power counts beside its bracket (D-774 to D-777).** 🔧 built 2026-09-20, as #196. It came after #195, which merged PR-59.
The Gríma review of 2026-09-14 read the deck as bracket 3 by the rules, and the build aimed at bracket 5. The profile measured each power feature of the deck, and no number of the app told the reader. The deck page showed "Bracket 5" and stopped.

- **The source.** `ProfileFeature` of `proto/mtg/v1/deck.proto` already carries `value`, `low`, `high`, and `has_high` for each power feature. The server writes it, and `GetDeck` returns it. No file of `web/apps/web/src` read `deck.profile` before this change.
- **The floors.** A bracket of 4 or 5 sets a floor for tutors, fast mana, and Game Changers. The row reads "Tutors 0 of 4" (D-774).
- **The caps.** Bracket 1 to 3 sets a cap for the same three features. The row reads "Tutors 1 of 5 at most" (D-775). Without the cap the reader of most decks saw no count at all.
- **The plan.** The finisher floor holds at every bracket, and the bracket system counts no finisher. So the finisher row names the deck plan and never the bracket (D-743, D-776).
- **The surface.** The deck page alone shows the counts, and the chat page renders the same component (D-777). The change needs no protobuf field and no server change.

Gate: a Commander deck of bracket 5 shows four counts beside its bracket, and each missed floor reads "short". A deck of bracket 3 shows three caps, and a cap the deck breaks reads "over". A deck with no profile shows no count, and a 60-card deck shows none. A feature the profile did not measure gets no row. Five of the six new tests fail on the old code, and the sixth is a negative guard.

2026-09-20 (built): six new tests cover the helper and the deck page. `powerCounts` of `web/apps/web/src/features/deck/deck-stats.ts` reads the profile alone. So the counts need no card data and no second network read. No paid target ran, and no Go file changes.
> *In plain English:* the app told the reader the bracket of a deck and no more. Two decks of the same bracket can hold very different power. The page now shows how many tutors, fast mana, and Game Changers the deck holds. It shows how many the bracket wants beside each one. A number the deck misses reads "short".

**PR-61: A deck build over the API alone, with no GUI (D-778 to D-781).** 🔧 built 2026-09-20.
The live check of #196 read the deploy, the released chunks, and the power counts of the deck page. It read no built deck. `scripts/read-session.sh` reads one session by id, and the sandbox of a session refuses every other read under `users/<uid>` (D-638). No session opens a browser as the owner, so a deck page needed the owner's hand. So no session had a way to prove that the deployed app still builds a deck.

- **The client.** `go/cmd/api-build` is the first command of this repo that calls the deployed API as a client. `go/cmd/chat-probe` builds `agentsvc` in memory behind a test server, so it proves the RPC path and no part of the deployed stack.
- **The token.** The deployed project enables the email and password provider alone. So `accounts:signInWithPassword` of Identity Toolkit gives the command an id token with no browser. The interceptor of `go/internal/auth/auth.go` verifies that token, and it then reads the invite list.
- **The account.** A dedicated check account signs in, and never the account of the owner (D-779). The counters of D-638 count a creation and never lower.
- **The flow.** One run imports a ManaBox CSV, sends the prompt, answers each question, and reads the deck back with `GetDeck`, `ListDecks`, and `GetSession`. A deck in the stream proves the build. Only a deck that comes back out of storage proves that a reader finds it.
- **The answers.** `-answers` names one reply for each slot. A slot with no entry takes the first option of a closed question (D-295), and it declines the rest (D-353). A `no_decline` question takes its first option (D-690). So the run never stops and waits for a person.
- **The cost.** The deployed API calls the real providers, so the target is a paid one (D-781). `API_BUILD=1` guards it.

Gate: `make api-build` signs in, imports the collection, answers every question, and reads the built deck back out of the deployed project. The run prints the deck id, the session id, and the collection id. A run with no account, a wrong password, or an account off the invite list fails with the reason and the command that fixes it. The free tests drive the whole flow against a test server, and the bearer test fails when the client sends no token.

2026-09-20 (built): twenty-one tests cover the answer plan and the whole flow. No test reaches the deployed project. `docs/reference/api-deck-build-2026-09-20.md` holds the method, the result of the live run, and the limits.
> *In plain English:* a session saw that the app deployed. It had no way to see that the app still works. Only a person with a browser built a deck and looked at it. This change gives a session a command that does the whole job. The command signs in, uploads a collection, asks for a deck, answers the questions, and reads the finished deck back. It uses its own account, so nothing of the owner moves.

**PR-62: The collection page scrolls once, with a pinned toolbar and a return to the top of the binder (F-167, D-806 to D-809).** ✅ merged as #211. The owner approved the plan before the code.
The collection page holds two scroll areas. `<main>` scrolls the page (D-364, D-625), and the binder grid scrolls a box of `max-h-[75vh]` inside it. The virtualizer, the column count, the reset after a new filter or sort, and the load of the next page all read that box.

- **One scroll area.** The binder grid loses `max-h-[75vh]` and `overflow-y-auto`. The virtualizer reads `<main>` through `closest("main")` of the grid, and through no global query.
- **The offset.** The grid starts below the page header, the upload cards, and the hero. `scrollMargin` holds that offset. A resize observer on the page measures it again when the content above the grid changes, for example when the hero loads.
- **The columns.** The column count reads the width of the grid box, as before.
- **The reset.** A new filter or sort moves `<main>` to the top of the binder only when the reader is below that point. A reader above the binder stays in place.
- **The next page.** `onReachEnd` reads the last virtual row, as before, so it needs no change.
- **The pinned toolbar.** The title row and the search box hold `sticky top-0` in `<main>`, with the page background and a lower border (D-807). At `md` and wider, the five selects pin below them.
- **The phone.** Under `md` the five selects scroll away, so the pinned block stays small at 390 px. A resize observer gives the selects the height of the first block as their offset.
- **The button.** "Back to the binder top" appears after the reader scrolls one screen below the top of the binder (D-808). It holds `sticky bottom-4` at the end of the binder section, so it stays inside the page area.
- **The move.** A click moves `<main>` to the top of the binder, and it moves focus to the binder heading. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the move is instant, else it is smooth. The virtualizer makes the move. A row that it measures on the way up corrects the scroll, and that correction stops a smooth scroll of another source.
- **Out of scope.** The chat page keeps its scroll areas (D-806).

Gate: a new Playwright spec in `web/apps/web/e2e` runs in `make smoke` at 390 by 844 and at 1280 by 800. It uploads the ManaBox test file of 2,547 rows.

- One element of the page scrolls up and down, and it is `<main>`.
- After a scroll of three screens, the toolbar sits at the top of `<main>`, and the search box shows.
- At 390 px the pinned block holds 25 percent of the viewport height or less.
- The next page of rows loads when the reader comes near the end.
- The button shows after the scroll, and not before.
- A click puts the toolbar and the first row on the screen, and focus on the binder heading.
- Under reduced motion the move ends at once.
- `web/apps/web/e2e/phone.spec.ts` and `make verify` still pass.
- Vitest tests cover the reset rule, the button threshold, and the reduced motion choice, and each fails on the old code.

After the merge, the next session reads the live chunk of `decktome.com` for the button label and for the absence of `max-h-[75vh]`. Then it runs the spec on `decktome.com` as the check account of D-779 (D-809). `SMOKE_BASE_URL`, `API_BUILD_EMAIL`, and `API_BUILD_PASSWORD` select that run.

2026-09-22 (built): `web/apps/web/e2e/collection-scroll.spec.ts` passes at both widths in `make smoke`. Against the old grid it fails at its first check, "more than one element scrolls up and down". Four of the five new Vitest tests fail on the old grid. The fifth is a negative guard, as a reader above the binder stays in place on both. The first spec run found the virtualizer fault of the move, and the fix above holds. The column count now reads the rows box when that box appears. The old observer ran one time at mount, before the first page gave a box. No paid target ran.
> *In plain English:* the collection page has a scrollbar for the page and a second one for the card grid inside it. The reader scrolls one, then the other, and the search box leaves the screen. After this change the page has one scrollbar. The search box stays at the top while the cards move. A button takes the reader back to the first card in one click.

**PR-63: A Codex review gates each pull request, and a change of documents skips the heavy CI jobs (D-810 to D-822).** ✅ merged as #212. The mark came after the first Codex review, and D-822 moves it before the Gitar pass.
The owner asked for the review gate of what-you-carry and the-thing-below (D-810). Before this item, Gitar was the only review, and every pull request ran every job of `verify` (D-639).

- **The review order.** Gitar reviews each push, and the author answers each finding. Then a Codex session writes `docs/reviews/pr-<number>.md` with the new `pr-review` skill (D-811).
- **The check.** `.github/workflows/review-gate.yml` runs `docs/tools/review_gate.py` from `main` on `pull_request_target`. It reads the head as data, with a token that reads contents alone (D-816).
- **The rules.** The record exists, its verdict is `Ready for owner merge`, and its head field names the effective head (D-813). A commit of the record or the hand-off alone keeps the approval.
- **The label.** `review-override` passes a pull request of documents alone. The session applies it when the pull request is ready and every other check is green (D-812, D-814).
- **Dependabot.** A pull request of Dependabot passes when Dependabot wrote every commit (D-817).
- **The ruleset.** The ruleset `review-gate` of `main` requires the check, with no bypass (D-815).
- **The CI skip.** A new job `verify:skip` runs `docs/tools/ci_skip.py` from the base commit (D-818 to D-820). The six heavy jobs skip under rule 1 or rule 2 of D-818. The `shell` and `eval` jobs always run.

Gate:

- `docs/tools/test_review_gate.py` covers each rule, the label set, the Dependabot guard, and a real git history, with a move of code into `docs/`.
- `docs/tools/test_ci_skip.py` covers both skip rules, a red, cancelled, or absent run, and the search for the last change of code.
- Each test file reads the workflow that it guards, so a change of the job names fails a test.
- A live read of the rules against #211 gives the expected result for each case.
- A Codex record of this pull request approves its effective head (D-821).
- `make verify` passes.

GitHub starts `pull_request_target` from `main` alone, so the check can not run on this pull request. The first live run of both rules is the next pull request. The session disables the ruleset for this merge, and enables it again after the merge (D-815).
> *In plain English:* until now, one automatic reviewer read each change, and every change ran the full test suite. After this change, a second AI from another company reads each change of code. GitHub refuses the merge until its written verdict approves the latest code. A change of documents alone skips the slow tests, but only when the code under it already passed them.

**PR-64: The author session starts the Codex review, and a pull request merges itself on the green light (D-823 to D-834).** ✅ merged as #213. The mark comes before the Gitar pass (D-822).
PR-63 made the Codex record a required check. The owner still started each Codex review in the desktop app, and the owner merged each pull request (D-583, D-811).

- **The target.** `make codex-review PR=<n>` runs `docs/tools/codex_review.py`. It updates the npm CLI, checks the login and the model, and runs `codex exec` in a new worktree at the head (D-823 to D-825).
- **The guards.** The target refuses a closed pull request, a checkout that differs from origin, a dirty tree, and an incomplete Gitar pass. A refusal spends nothing (D-832).
- **The read.** After the review, the target reads the record from origin, and it checks the head field against the effective head. Each outcome has its own exit code (D-832).
- **The three-strike stop.** Each finding lists the heads at which a review found it open. The third head of a blocking finding stops the loop for the owner (D-826).
- **No API key.** Each Codex call runs with no API key in its environment, and it needs a ChatGPT login (D-833).
- **The auto-merge.** The ruleset of `main` requires each pull request job of `verify`, `pr-contract`, `review-gate`, and each resolved thread. The squash merge is the one method, and the auto-merge is on (D-828, D-830). The owner confirms each merge after a summary of one paragraph (D-834).
- **The files of the rules.** `.github/rulesets/` holds the ruleset and the merge settings, and `make ruleset-check` compares them with GitHub.

Gate:

- `docs/tools/test_codex_review.py` covers each refusal, the three rounds of D-826, and a fixed finding that opens again. It also covers each outcome, and the read over a real git history.
- `docs/tools/test_ruleset_check.py` compares the ruleset file with the job names of `.github/workflows/verify.yml`.
- `make ruleset-check` passes after the change of the settings.
- Each required check reports on a head of documents alone and on a head of code.
- `make codex-review` approves the effective head of this pull request, and this pull request merges itself (D-829).
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* until now, the owner started each second review by hand and pressed the merge button. After this change, the working session starts the review with one command. When every check is green and the review approves, GitHub merges the change. When a review finds the same problem three times, the loop stops and asks the owner.

**PR-65: A commit of documents alone keeps a green review gate green (D-837).** ✅ merged as #215. The mark comes before the Gitar pass (D-822).
RG 5 of PR-63 read the effective head of D-813 alone. So a later commit of the roadmap, a decision, or a skill turned the check red, and it asked for a new Codex review.

- **The rule.** RG 5 of `docs/tools/review_gate.py` also passes a record of an earlier commit, when each later commit changes documents alone. The documents are the set of D-814.
- **The limits.** A path of code, a refused path of D-814, or a merge commit after the recorded head still fails the check.
- **What stays.** A reviewer still records the effective head of D-813. `make codex-review` still waits for the Gitar pass of each later commit, and Gitar still reviews each commit of documents (D-752).

Gate:

- `docs/tools/test_review_gate.py` covers each kind of document, many commits of documents, a later commit of code, a refused path, and a merge. It also covers a pull request of documents alone.
- Each test that expects a pass fails on the rule of `11a30e7`.
- The `review-gate` check reads its rule from `main` (D-816). So this pull request runs the old rule on its own head, and the tests prove the change.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* until now, any edit after the second review, even one line of text, asked for that review again. After this change, an edit of documents alone keeps the approval. The first review bot still reads each edit, and a change of code still needs the second review.

**PR-66: The Gitar requirement pauses, and `make codex-review` takes `--skip-gitar-review` (D-838).** ✅ merged as #216. The mark comes before any review (D-822).
The owner paused the required Gitar review until a later pull request ends the pause. The owner asked for a change that is easy to reverse.

- **The switch.** `docs/reference/gitar-pause.md` holds the rules and the steps of the end. Each rule document holds one pause note with the same bold text.
- **The flag.** `make codex-review PR=<n> -- --skip-gitar-review` reads no Gitar pass. It still refuses an open review thread or an issue on the Gitar dashboard. The flag stays after the pause.
- **The feedback cycle.** While the pause file exists, step 8 needs no Gitar review. An open thread or a dashboard issue stops the cycle before the fixer, and a comment tells the owner.
- **What stays.** Each Gitar finding still gets its answer. A Gitar finding stops the work, and the session tells the owner at once.

Gate:

- `docs/tools/test_codex_review.py` covers the flag: an open thread or a dashboard issue refuses, and the flag skips the Gitar pass. It also covers each clean form of the stored dashboards, and each unknown form.
- `docs/tools/test_gitar_pause.py` fails when a pause note stays without the pause file, or when a rule document holds no note during the pause.
- `go/internal/triage/loop_test.go` runs the review round with a fake `gh`. A thread or a dashboard issue stops it with 3 and a comment to the owner.
- `make codex-review` passes the flag, and it refuses an unknown flag before the review.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* the first review bot no longer holds up a change. The session starts the second review with a new flag. When the first bot still writes a comment, the session stops and tells the owner. To undo the pause, delete one file and the marked notes.

**PR-67: A bracket 5 shortlist reads the card rate of its commander (F-166, D-839).** ✅ merged as #217. The mark comes before any review (D-822).
The judge read the Najeela deck of bracket gate runs 9 and 10 at bracket 4, for its warrior filler. The rate of the format can not tell which cards a commander plays, and it counts no land. `docs/reference/f166-commander-rate-2026-09-23.md` holds each measurement.

- **The rate.** The fit reads each TopDeck list before the tier cap. A commander or a pair with 10 lists or more gets the share of its lists that hold each card, lands included. A card under 0.1 has no row.
- **The shortlist.** A bracket 5 Commander request with a rate ranks on it first. The theme adds 0.05 times its score, the owner choice after a free sweep. A card the lists of the commander play stays on the list with the signal "commander rate".
- **What stays.** Brackets 1 to 4 and the other formats read no commander rate. A commander with no row, and a pair with no row of its own, rank as before. The deployed app reads the rates after the first meta job that runs the new fit.

Gate:

- `go/internal/quality/commander_rates_test.go` covers the list floor, the rate floor, the basic lands, the other sources, the pair key, and the round trip.
- `go/internal/candidates/commander_rate_test.go` covers the shortlist: bracket 5 keeps an off-theme card of the commander lists over a theme card. Bracket 4, no bracket, and Standard read no rate. The test fails with the lever off.
- Bracket gate run 11 reads prompts 13 to 15 at bracket 5, 3 of 3 in each of two judge lanes, for $0.3356. Each deck sits in every band.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* a request for a top-power deck with a theme got theme cards that real tournament decks never play. The builder now starts from the cards that winning decks of the same commander play, and the theme adds a little. Other power levels do not change.

**PR-68: The first harvest, and the triage leaves out a case that no gate can run (F-49, D-840, D-841).** ✅ merged as #218. The mark comes before any review (D-822).
The first harvest read 4 verdicts of one user. One verdict carries a snapshot, and it is a C4 defect with no gate case. The dry cycle still counted 2 conversation cases, and neither holds a message. `docs/reference/f49-harvest-2026-09-23.md` holds each measurement.

- **The rule.** A conversation case with no message gets a reason in `NoRun`. The manifest and `-apply` leave it out, and the report names it.
- **The judge lane.** One live triage cost $0.0122. The judge put the Aragorn verdict in C4, but #99 fixed that fault the same day (F-75).

- **The Gitar notice.** A Gitar comment that names no specific item is not a finding (D-842). The reviewer and the author ignore it.

Gate:

- `TestACaseWithNoMessageReachesNoGate` and `TestApplyWritesNoCaseThatNoGateRuns` fail without the rule.
- `make feedback-loop-dry` on the harvest reads 0 cases a gate measures, and the cycle stops.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* the loop that turns complaints into fixes read its first real complaints. None of them held enough detail to test, so it now says so and spends nothing on them.

**PR-69: The land cap of a bracket 5 shortlist keeps the core lands of its commander (F-168, F-169, D-843, D-844).** ✅ merged as #219. The mark comes before any review (D-822).
The land cap kept 20 lands in the mana order, and that order reads no commander rate. So the Najeela shortlist held 7 pain lands that no Najeela list plays, and it dropped four lands that most of the lists play. `docs/reference/f168-land-cap-2026-09-23.md` holds each measurement.

- **The order.** A bracket 5 Commander request with a commander rate fills the mana half of the land cap in score order. The half keeps its 20 places, and the theme half does not change.
- **The fill and the cut.** The fill of an owned-first list caps its lands in the same order. The total cut of D-835 keeps the mana half first, in the same order.
- **What stays.** Brackets 1 to 4, the other formats, and a commander with no rate row keep the order of D-450 and D-733.
- **The hands floor.** The floor of first hands with two to four lands reads 0.56 at brackets 4 and 5, the low quarter of the TopDeck lists (D-844). The land band stays 27 to 33.

Gate:

- `go/internal/candidates/land_cap_rate_test.go` covers the cap, the owned-first fill, the total cut, and bracket 4. A revert of each of the three parts of the change fails its own case.
- A free sweep of the 231 commanders with a rate cuts the core lands that a shortlist drops from 163 to 9.
- Bracket gate run 12 reads prompt 15 for $0.1082, and a second judge lane reads it for $0.0167. Both judge reads give bracket 5. The verdict reads FAIL on the old hands floor alone, at 0.60 against 0.65, and the new floor is 0.56.
- `go/internal/profile/prompt_bands_test.go` reads the new floor in the prompt lines of brackets 4 and 5, and it fails on the old bands.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* a top-power deck got lands that no winning deck of its commander plays. It lost lands that most of those decks play. The land list now follows the same winning decks. The check of the first hands also failed most real winning decks. Its bar moves to what those decks play.

**PR-70: Import a deck list, and show it as a deck the app built (F-170, F-171, D-845 to D-862).** ✅ merged as #220. The mark comes before any review (D-822). The owner approved the scope on 2026-09-23 (D-860).
A user brings a deck of their own, and the app stores it as a deck that a build made. So the deck page shows it, and the revise turn changes it. The owner asked for the import on 2026-09-23.

- **The input.** The page `/decks` gets an import form. It takes a text file that Archidekt exports, or a pasted Arena list (D-845). The app fetches no URL.
- **The parse.** A deck parser reads the Archidekt line `1x Name (set) number *F* [Category]` and the Arena sections `Commander`, `Deck`, `Sideboard`, and `Companion`. It also reads the line `// COMMANDER`, and a blank line ends that section. It resolves each name as a collection upload does, and it reports each name that matches no card.
- **The format.** A commander mark or a main deck of 100 cards reads Commander. For any other list, the user picks Standard, Modern, or neither, and neither stores as the house format (D-857).
- **The commander.** The Archidekt category `Commander{top}`, the Arena header `Commander`, or the line `// COMMANDER` names it. With neither mark, the user picks from the cards of the list that can lead a deck (D-847).
- **The checks.** Each card stays as the user wrote it, and the page shows each finding of `rules.Validate` (D-846). The roles come from `assignRole` alone (D-848).
- **The collection.** A user with a collection can pick one to show the owned cards and the buy list (D-849).
- **The bracket.** `Profiler.Floor` reads the floor, and `JudgeBracket` reads the bracket. The bracket never sits under the floor (D-850). When the judge fails, the deck stores the floor as an estimate, and the next open reads again (D-854). A 60-card import shows that the app did not read its power step yet (D-859).
- **The summary.** The reason of the judge and the quality sentence (D-855).
- **The session.** The import writes a session with the format, the commander, the colors, the bracket, and the collection. Its theme stays empty. The revise turn then reads the deck as it reads a generated deck (D-851).
- **The user record and the thumbs.** A new counter `total_decks_imported` counts each import, and the import session counts as a session (D-852, D-862). The thumbs stay off on the import and apply to each revision (D-853).
- **The backfill.** A stored deck holds the flat fields `imported` and `revised_from_deck_id`, so the backfill counts an import and a revision apart (F-171, D-861).
- **The name.** An Archidekt import takes the name of its file, and the form names a pasted list.
- **The cost.** One judge read for each Commander import. Bracket gate run 12 read one deck for $0.0167, and run 9 read six for $0.0833. A 60-card import calls no model. The spend cap of D-421 covers the import.

Gate:

- The parser reads `living_weapon.txt`, the Archidekt export of the owner (D-856): 100 cards, with Ekthi, Contaminator Priest as the commander. The commander earns a finding of legality. The foil line, the double-faced names, and the collector number `XLN-235` resolve.
- The parser reads `Turtle_power.txt`, the Arena export of the owner (D-860): 100 cards, with Heroes in a Half Shell as the commander.
- A test of the parser reads an Arena list with each of the four sections.
- Tests of the import cover the session, a revise turn on the imported deck, the new counter, and the hidden thumbs.
- Tests of the import also cover the floor on a judge failure and the house format.
- `go/internal/generate/import_test.go` covers the floor over a lower judge answer, a higher judge answer, a judge failure, a 60-card list, and the owned marks.
- `go/internal/feedbacksvc/service_test.go` refuses a verdict on an import and takes one on its revision.
- `go/cmd/users-backfill/main_test.go` reads a revision and an import from the flat fields and from an older packed deck.
- Tests of the web form cover the paste, the file, the collection pick, the format pick, and the commander pick.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* today the app shows only the decks it built. This item lets you bring a deck you already have, from an Archidekt file or a pasted Arena list. The app shows it with the same checks, reads its bracket, and changes it on request.

**PR-71: The power step of a 60-card import (F-170, F-172, D-858, D-859, D-863 to D-876).** ✅ merged as #221.
A judge reads the power step of a 60-card import: casual, FNM level, or tournament. REFUTED 2026-09-23 (D-863): "no source on record labels a casual list or an FNM list". The product types of the precon table label a casual list. No product type marks the FNM line, so a model of another family labeled the FNM lists (D-874, D-875).

- **The judge.** `JudgeSixtyStep` in `go/internal/generate/judge.go` reads the main deck, the sideboard, and the format word. Each card line holds the rules text of the card (D-872). It answers the step and a reason, one judge call for each import. The prompt defines each step by its anchor (D-866).
- **The import.** `ReadImport` asks the judge for each import that is not Commander. The step stands with no guard (D-865). The profile and the grade then read the deck at that step, as a generated 60-card deck does (D-869). The reason of the judge and the quality sentence make the summary (D-855).
- **The failure.** The deck stores no step, and the page shows that the app did not read it yet (D-864). The deck page asks again at each open while an imported 60-card deck holds no step. So an import stored before this item also gets its read.
- **The new read.** `ReadImportBracket` reads the step of a 60-card deck and the bracket of a Commander deck. The RPC keeps its name, because the web and the API deploy apart. A new read also writes the power into the power slot of the session, because a revise turn reads the slot (F-172, D-870).
- **The labels.** Casual reads the Theme, Intro, and Planeswalker decks of the precon table. FNM reads the user decks of MTGGoldfish that a model outside the family of the judge labeled fnm (D-873 to D-875). Tournament reads the top 8 of an MTGO Challenge and of an MTGTop8 RCQ (D-863). It takes 13 MTGO lists and 12 MTGTop8 lists, with at most two lists of one event (D-871). An RCQ counts from 32 players (D-876).
- **The pick.** Each rung, or each source of a rung, takes its newest lists that resolve whole, by date, then by key. Every fifth list of a rung makes the dev split, and the other 20 make the test split (D-867). The newest 25 casual lists run from 2019-01 to 2026-04, so the age of their cards can confound the step. The FNM rung holds 9 Modern and 16 Standard decks of September 2026.
- **The format word.** A casual precon reads Standard, the format of its release. A user deck or a tournament list reads Modern or Standard.
- **The target.** `make sixty-gate` reads the test split three times and writes a gate document. It has the guard `SIXTY_GATE=1`, and any file at `SIXTY_GATE_OUT` stops the run. The flag `-exclude` leaves out the tournament lists of earlier gate documents (D-876). `make sixty-gate-dry` prints the split for free.
- **The cost.** Test run 2 read 180 times for $3.9736, so a read costs about $0.022, and so does each 60-card import. The dev lanes and the two test runs cost $8.9265 in all.

Gate:

- `make sixty-gate` reads the step of the label on 80 percent of the test reads, and no read sits two steps off (D-868). Run 1 read FAIL, 133 of 180, on RCQs of 16 to 24 players. Run 2 read PASS, 148 of 180, with fresh tournament lists (D-876).
- Tests of `JudgeSixtyStep` cover the schema, each step, a bad answer, and the rules text of each card.
- Tests of the import cover the step, the profile at the step, and a judge failure with no step, no profile, and no grade. Tests of the new read cover the power slot of the session (F-172).
- Tests of the deck page cover the new read of a 60-card import with no step. A deck with a step gets no new read.
- Tests of `go/cmd/sixty-gate` cover the pick, the split, the cap of each event, the RCQ size, the exclusion, and the verdict.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* a Commander import gets its power read, and a 60-card import does not yet. This item adds that read. It tests the reader against Wizards decks for casual play and against tournament winners. For Friday Night Magic, another model rated real user decks.

**PR-72: The feedback fix cycle follows the one-PR rule and the Codex review (F-49, D-877 to D-879).** ✅ merged as #222.
Before this item the cycle cut its own branch and opened its own pull request. So a session that drove it worked on two pull requests (D-746). Its review step read Gitar alone and waited for it, although D-838 pauses Gitar. No Codex review read a pull request of the cycle, so `review-gate` refused its merge.

- **The branch.** The flag `--here` of `scripts/feedback-loop.sh` commits the case, the fix, and the evidence on the branch of the session. It pushes nothing (D-877). The session writes the documents and opens the pull request. A failed cycle names the commit that drops its work.
- **The review step.** `scripts/feedback-review.sh` waits for the checks of the tip and reads Gitar one time during the pause. It then runs `docs/tools/codex_review.py`. The fixer answers each Codex finding in the response file, and the round reverts a fix that touches a frozen path or the record (D-878).
- **No merge.** No script of the cycle merges or turns on the auto-merge. The fixer runs with no GitHub login. The owner decides the merge in the session that drove the cycle (D-878).
- **The harvest.** `make feedback-harvest` writes under `.local/feedback` (D-879). A case keeps the words of the reader (D-642).
- **The thumbs down of 2026-09-24.** It names a card of a Commander deck with the reason `wrong_power` and the words of the reader. It holds the snapshot of D-635, with the session and the deck. The free triage puts it in C5, and it wants one judge read for the words. The harvest stays under `.local`.

Gate:

- `TestHereCommitsOnTheBranchOfTheSession` runs a copy of the cycle in a new repository. It cuts no branch, it refuses `main` and a detached HEAD, and a failure names the reset.
- `TestTheReviewStepReadsGitarOnceThenCodex` runs the review step with a fake `gh`, `git`, and Codex review. A pending Gitar check holds no round of the pause, and a failed check stops the round before Codex. An approval pulls the record and ends with 0, and a three-strike stop ends with 4.
- `TestTheCycleNeverMerges` and `TestTheHarvestWritesUnderLocal` hold the rest. Each new test fails on the scripts of `3b2f686`.
- `make verify` passes.
> *In plain English:* when a reader complains, the app can turn the complaint into a test and a fix. This change keeps that work in one reviewed pull request, and only the owner can merge it.

**PR-73: One live fix cycle on the thumbs down of 2026-09-24 (F-49, F-173, F-174, D-877, D-878, D-880, D-881).** ✅ merged as #223.
The thumbs down of 2026-09-24 carries a session and a deck, so the triage can write a case that a gate runs. The judge lane put it in C5, the power of one card, and the case went to the bracket gate.

- **The session.** It made the branch `feedback-live-cycle` from `main` and drove the cycle with `--here`. Its own pull request holds the evidence, the fix of F-173, and the documents (D-877).
- **The paid steps.** The owner approved each step first. The judge lane cost $0.0099. The cycle cost $0.0057 of triage and $0.2135 of two bracket gate runs, under a cap of $2.
- **The result.** Case 16 passed before the fix. Its two builds held 20 fixing lands, and the judge agreed with bracket 4. The fixer made no change. The owner dropped case 16, because it measured nothing of the complaint (D-880).
- **F-173.** The cycle still ran the fixer and a measure run, and it ended with 0. `case-check` now exits 3 when no case fails before the fix, and the cycle stops before the fixer.
- **F-174.** The live deck read the pool rule owned-only, and a bracket case builds with any card. M-19 replays the shortlist for free (D-881).

- The case fails before the fix and passes after it, and `make eval-check` shows no flip. Not met on 2026-09-24: case 16 passed before the fix (D-880).

The cycle of 2026-09-24 is a failed cycle of D-877: its confirm run read that 1 of 1 cases did not read fail. It ended with 0 only because of F-173, and `TestTheCycleStopsWhenNoCaseFailsBeforeTheFix` proves that the fixed cycle ends with 1. The session dropped the case (D-880), and `docs/reference/feedback/fix-cycle-20260924-153731.md` records the cost and the reason.
